^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  112) def joinstem(cutpoint: int | None = 0, words: Iterable[str] | None = None) -> str:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  113)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  114)     Join stem of each word in words into a string for regex.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  115) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  116)     Each word is truncated at cutpoint.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  117) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  118)     Cutpoint is usually negative indicating the number of letters to remove
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  119)     from the end of each word.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  120) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  121)     >>> joinstem(-2, ["ephemeris", "iris", ".*itis"])
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  122)     '(?:ephemer|ir|.*it)'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  123) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  124)     >>> joinstem(None, ["ephemeris"])
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  125)     '(?:ephemeris)'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  126) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  127)     >>> joinstem(5, None)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  128)     '(?:)'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  129)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  130)     return enclose("|".join(w[:cutpoint] for w in words or []))
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  131) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  132) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  133) def bysize(words: Iterable[str]) -> dict[int, set]:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  134)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  135)     From a list of words, return a dict of sets sorted by word length.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  136) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  137)     >>> words = ['ant', 'cat', 'dog', 'pig', 'frog', 'goat', 'horse', 'elephant']
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  138)     >>> ret = bysize(words)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  139)     >>> sorted(ret[3])
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  140)     ['ant', 'cat', 'dog', 'pig']
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  141)     >>> ret[5]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  142)     {'horse'}
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  143)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  144)     res: dict[int, set] = collections.defaultdict(set)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  145)     for w in words:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  146)         res[len(w)].add(w)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  147)     return res
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  148) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  149) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  150) def make_pl_si_lists(
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  151)     lst: Iterable[str],
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  152)     plending: str,
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  153)     siendingsize: int | None,
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  154)     dojoinstem: bool = True,
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  155) ):
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  156)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  157)     given a list of singular words: lst
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  158) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  159)     an ending to append to make the plural: plending
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  160) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  161)     the number of characters to remove from the singular
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  162)     before appending plending: siendingsize
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  163) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  164)     a flag whether to create a joinstem: dojoinstem
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  165) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  166)     return:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  167)     a list of pluralised words: si_list (called si because this is what you need to
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  168)     look for to make the singular)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  169) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  170)     the pluralised words as a dict of sets sorted by word length: si_bysize
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  171)     the singular words as a dict of sets sorted by word length: pl_bysize
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  172)     if dojoinstem is True: a regular expression that matches any of the stems: stem
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  173)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  174)     if siendingsize is not None:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  175)         siendingsize = -siendingsize
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  176)     si_list = [w[:siendingsize] + plending for w in lst]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  177)     pl_bysize = bysize(lst)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  178)     si_bysize = bysize(si_list)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  179)     if dojoinstem:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  180)         stem = joinstem(siendingsize, lst)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  181)         return si_list, si_bysize, pl_bysize, stem
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  182)     else:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  183)         return si_list, si_bysize, pl_bysize
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200  184)

^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2022) def _grouped_from_right(digits: str, size: int = 3) -> Iterator[str]:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2023)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2024)     Yield ``digits`` in groups of ``size`` characters, aligned from the right.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2025) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2026)     >>> list(_grouped_from_right("1234567"))
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2027)     ['1', '234', '567']
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2028)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2029)     head = (len(digits) - 1) % size + 1
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2030)     yield digits[:head]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2031)     yield from (digits[i : i + size] for i in range(head, len(digits), size))
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2032) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2033) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2034) def _passthrough_sign(func: Callable[..., str]) -> Callable[..., str]:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2035)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2036)     Wrap a magnitude transform so a leading ``+``/``-`` sign passes through.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2037) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2038)     The sign is stripped from the first argument before the call and restored
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2039)     on the result, so ``func`` only ever sees an unsigned magnitude.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2040) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2041)     >>> _passthrough_sign(str.upper)("-abc")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2042)     '-ABC'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2043)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2044) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2045)     @functools.wraps(func)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2046)     def wrapper(text: str, *args: object, **kwargs: object) -> str:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2047)         sign = text[:1] if text[:1] in "+-" else ""
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2048)         return sign + func(text[len(sign) :], *args, **kwargs)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2049) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2050)     return wrapper
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2051) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2052) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2053) @_passthrough_sign
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2054) def _group_thousands(integer_text: str, separator: str) -> str:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2055)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2056)     Insert ``separator`` between groups of three digits, from the right.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2057) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2058)     >>> _group_thousands("1234567", "_")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2059)     '1_234_567'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2060)     >>> _group_thousands("-1000", ",")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2061)     '-1,000'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2062)     """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2063)     return separator.join(_grouped_from_right(integer_text))

^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2409)             else:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2410)                 result = inflected.split(" ")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2411)                 for index, word in enumerate(result):
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2412)                     if "|" in word:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2413)                         result[index] = word.split("|")[self.classical_dict["all"]]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2414)         else:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2415)             result = inflected.split(" ")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2416) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2417)         # Try to fix word wise capitalization by restoring each inflected
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2418)         # word's case from the original word it came from. Inflecting a phrase
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2419)         # can change the number of words (e.g. "a cat" -> "cats" drops the
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2420)         # article), so pair the words positionally only as far as they align
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2421)         # and leave any surplus inflected words untouched rather than indexing
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2422)         # past the end of either sequence.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2423)         originals = orig.split(" ")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2424)         recased = (
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2425)             self._recase(original, word) for original, word in zip(originals, result)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2426)         )
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2427)         surplus = result[len(originals) :]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2428)         return " ".join(itertools.chain(recased, surplus))
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2429) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2430)     def _recase(self, original: str, inflected: str) -> str:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2431)         """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2432)         Return ``inflected`` recased to match the case of ``original``.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2433)         """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2434)         if original in self.pl_sb_preserve_case:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2435)             # This word's capitalization is intrinsic (e.g. "I", or an
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2436)             # acronym registered via ``defnoun(..., case='preserve')``), so
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2437)             # keep the inflected case rather than normalizing it to the case
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2438)             # of the input.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2439)             return inflected
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2440)         if original == original.upper():
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2441)             return inflected.upper()
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2442)         if original == original.capitalize():
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2443)             return inflected.capitalize()
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2444)         return inflected
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2445) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2446)     def partition_word(self, text: str) -> tuple[str, str, str]:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2447)         mo = PARTITION_WORD.search(text)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2448)         if mo:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2449)             return mo.group(1), mo.group(2), mo.group(3)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2450)         else:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2451)             return "", "", ""
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 2452)

^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3043)     @classmethod
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3044)     def _handle_prepositional_phrase(cls, phrase, transform, sep):
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3045)         """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3046)         Given a word or phrase possibly separated by sep, parse out
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3047)         the prepositional phrase and apply the transform to the word
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3048)         preceding the prepositional phrase.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3049) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3050)         Raise ValueError if the pivot is not found or if at least two
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3051)         separators are not found.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3052) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3053)         >>> engine._handle_prepositional_phrase("man-of-war", str.upper, '-')
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3054)         'MAN-of-war'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3055)         >>> engine._handle_prepositional_phrase("man of war", str.upper, ' ')
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3056)         'MAN of war'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3057)         """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3058)         parts = phrase.split(sep)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3059)         if len(parts) < 3:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3060)             raise ValueError("Cannot handle words with fewer than two separators")
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3061) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3062)         pivot = cls._find_pivot(parts, pl_prep_list_da)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3063) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3064)         transformed = transform(parts[pivot - 1]) or parts[pivot - 1]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3065) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3066)         def assemble(head: str) -> str:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3067)             return " ".join(
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3068)                 parts[: pivot - 1] + [sep.join([head, parts[pivot], ''])]
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3069)             ) + " ".join(parts[(pivot + 1) :])
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3070) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3071)         # The transform may return a "modern|classical" alternation (e.g.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3072)         # "brothers|brethren"); distribute it across each variant so the pipe
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3073)         # stays at the top level for postprocess to resolve, instead of being
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3074)         # buried mid-word where the rest of the phrase would be dropped.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3075)         return "|".join(assemble(head) for head in str(transformed).split("|"))
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3076) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3077)     def _handle_long_compounds(self, word: Words, count: int) -> str | None:
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3078)         """
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3079)         Handles the plural and singular for compound `Words` that
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3080)         have three or more words, based on the given count.
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3081) 
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3082)         >>> engine()._handle_long_compounds(Words("pair of scissors"), 2)
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3083)         'pairs of scissors'
^74ba77b (evals 2026-10-22 12:38:58 +0200 3084)         >>> engine()._handle_long_compounds(Words("men beyond hills"), 1)
